ParlaMint-DK_20201215141519	The sitting is resumed. The debate is open. Sir. Jens Joel, the Social Democratic Party.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215141548	Thank you for that, President. This is just not just a regular Tuesday at the office. It's not just special because we're dealing with urgent matters. Unfortunately, we have done so many times with very good reason in 2020. But it is also because what we are embarking on now is the implementation of a really, really important agreement for our young people in vocational schools, for those who want to be skilled and for our labour market in the future. And I do not think that this case will fill as much in the Danish media landscape as it has actually deserved to do, because it is extremely important for us as a society, but not least for the young people it is about. It is about ensuring that there are enough apprenticeships for all those who actually apply and that it is not only those with the best networks that are the most well-known and who have an uncle or someone else in the family who are already a craftsman who is able to find an apprenticeship and complete their vocational training, but that it is actually all the others. That is why one of the major turns of the tripartite agreement that was concluded is that it is once and for all said that it is the responsibility of the community that it is not the 16-17-year-olds themselves who have to bear the task, but that it is the responsibility of the community, the schools, to ensure that there are indeed apprenticeships for the young people. You have to be very careful about promises in politics, and I would like to say that at least I came here to say something in the 2011 election campaign that has been following me ever since, but which I hope we can at some point clean up. Because in the 2011 election campaign we discussed this very much; I discussed it very much, and unfortunately we have been discussing it here for too many years. I came to say that if we couldn't solve this problem, I wasn't sure we would have deserved to vote for us again next time. I had to eat that in 2015 and 2019. Nevertheless, I hope that we are actually getting closer to solving this problem once and for all, or as some others would say: from scratch that terminology is otherwise used about something other than what this is about. And, as with so much else, we cannot solve this major social problem without reaching beyond Christiansborg, which is why I am very pleased that this is the end of a tripartite agreement with the social partners and the government. It has been extremely important for all parties to achieve this balance, but it has also been extremely important for us in the Social Democratic Party that when we found that balance, we also ensured that there was enough money to actually do the internship-seeking work and ensured that there were apprenticeships and that the commitments were put into schools and professional committees out there. So it's a really good day for all those who want to be skilled. It is a very good day for a society that needs more skilled people. And it is a very good day for the co-operative people's government and, by the way, the legislative part of what we also pride ourselves on, namely the Danish model, with a tripartite agreement that takes us a great step forward in the right direction. Thank you.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215141922	There is a brief comment on Mrs Ellen Trane Nørby. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215141926	Thank you. In all due respect for the rapporteur's need for a loud rhetoric, I would like to say that there is nothing in this draft law to solve the internship problem forever and ever. Well, this is a fine correction to the tripartite agreement we made in 2016 in relation to the element that is about balancing the AUB system. The savings have then been used to hold hands during some apprenticeships during the crisis, and this has also been important, but there is a need for quite different structural approaches if we are to solve, as such, the internship challenge that exists. ParlaMint-DK_20201215142656	Thank you for the floor. In many ways, it is a long-awaited bill that we are discussing today. It is a legislative proposal that finally balances the input and payouts of the AUB system. This is a matter we have discussed both in connection with L 60 last year and also before when the government was another, and also with Bill L 48 which will then be dealt with in parallel with this. The need to balance the AUB system is not only due to the fact that, in essence, this has been contrary to the legislation that has been in this area, as the Reichsaudit has also criticised. This is also due to the fact that the need became clearer when the corona epidemic hit Denmark in the spring, when company order books became thinner and thinner, employees were given a note and many apprentices and young people saw that the future that should be for them suddenly looked quite different. In a situation where company order books were thinner, there was also a need to ensure both the balance of the AUB system, but also to ensure that we used the savings that were in the AUB system to hold hands under the young. We proposed this on countless occasions during the spring, and so we were pleased that the tripartite agreement that was made finally ensured that the hand was held under the young. That is why it is also important that with this bill we ensure that we no longer get an overcharge from companies. It is both in the situation that we are still facing economically and that is challenged, but also forward-looking. As I said, we would have liked it to have happened earlier. If we then look at some of the elements that lie both in the tripartite agreement, but also in the draft law, in addition to the part that deals with the balance sheet, there is also the concern that, unfortunately, it is not a legislative proposal that creates more openness about the target figures and the internship situation. In other words, we do not understand why the Minister and the government did not take up the negotiations with the social partners. I also asked at the technical examination why it was not included in the draft law and got the answer that it was because it had not been brought up politically. This is a problem, because if we are to ensure that all young people are given an internship, it is not just a question of transferring more of the responsibility from the individual young people to greater responsibility for employers, as well as for schools and professional actors; it is also very much a question of transparency about who delivers and who does not deliver. This was also part of the essence of the 2016 agreement to ensure that those who did not lift the education responsibility had to pay in the future through additional collection. If you look at the bill then, as the Conservative rapporteur also said, the balance between public and private is shifting. This is something that worries us, because we do not have to do this simply because we move full employer responsibility in social and health matters from being shared between regions and municipalities to being isolated in the municipalities. After all, we are also at risk of pushing the balance between the responsibility of the public and private employers to create more internships. And the creation of more internships and the full transparency of who creates internships, who fulfils their training responsibilities, and who does not fulfil their training responsibilities, need to apply both to private employers and to public employers. If we look at the target figures and, as such, also at the target achievement and over-fulfilment that was in 2018, which unfortunately was not so good in 2019, compared to our goal of 8,000-10,000 additional internships until 2025, then we can see that it was particularly the public sector that did not deliver in the internship area. That is why we would have liked to see more openness in the legislative proposal we are discussing today regarding the establishment of work placements by public employers. If, for example, you're gonna have to... Looking at 2019 and at the companies, public as well as private, who paid an additional contribution because they did not lift their education responsibilities, the Capital Region paid 1.704.525 DKK. In addition. This is the expression of a great many young people who did not get the internship they should have had and for whom the Capital Region was then paid. DSB paid a little less, just about 1.5 million. kr. Post Denmark paid just under 1.5 million. kr. It is just to take some of the major public employers at both regional and state level. Therefore, our concern remains that this draft law does not change the structural nature of the fact that there are still too many people who do not lift their education responsibilities. If we look at the public sector alone, it is the case that if they had provided the target numbers set up for them, we would have had more than 4,000 additional internships in 2018 compared to what we had. It is thus excluded from the dimensional areas of social and health education and the PAU. That is why we need to create more openness and not just create the balance and shift of responsibility that lies in the current tripartite agreement and in the current legislative proposal. As I said, there are absolutely positive elements, but there are also elements that we do not wonder about in the legislative proposal. Thank you for the floor.

And when the covid-19 hit us and the business community had to shut down and a lot of young people suddenly got into trouble both on their vocational training and on their internship, it was, fortunately, that we got the decision that now business would have to stop their payments to the AUB for the rest of the year until we sort of solved this challenge with the AUB funds. Fortunately, the government and the minister took the initiative for tripartite negotiations • I think it was in August and now there is an agreement here in November, and that is welcome. It is gratifying that we have a system in which the parties can meet and land a common agreement. But we must not think that this solves all the problems of our vocational training, and there is also a small but small problem here, and it is, as a former rapporteur has said, that it is also important for the public to take responsibility for themselves in relation to their educational institutions. to a large extent the regions and municipalities, in addition to public enterprises as such. It is a little worrying that it says here that the regions can now actually be exempted from the responsibility for paying their additional contributions for these 1.780 trainees. They almost disappear from the inventory. It seems a little incomprehensible. However, the consequence may be that employers have to shoulder a relatively greater share of responsibility for training skilled workers, and we think that is the wrong signal to send out. It may also have to be transferred directly to the municipalities, but it does not actually say that they should take that responsibility. But they are automatically exempted. On the other hand, companies must, for example: in home care, on the other hand, manually apply for an additional contribution exemption, and it is somewhat inappropriate for such discrimination against business. We believe that it should be made more flexible and simpler and that there should be no discrimination between private and public authorities. Having said that, as far as our eud is concerned, we must not rest on the laurels and think that it is now running. There are many challenges. Yesterday we also saw how vulnerable young people find it difficult to maintain their education. There is only an implementation rate of 15 and there is a general dropout of 30-40 per cent. We are far from achieving the 20% target, and we are going to lack a large skilled labour force, blacksmiths, electricians and others to lift the green transition. So I really hope that together in the next year in 2021 we can have a proper discussion about the eud programmes and can create a little innovation around them and, of course, also how we can help those who need more time to implement them. Thank you for the floor.

For one of the times when the dropout in vocational training is the most important is between the basic course 2 and the start of the main course, when you start using your apprenticeship. This bill is actually a partial implementation of the tripartite agreement. Then a flexible AU contribution will be introduced, which means that we will levy the AU contributions necessary to finance the costs of the AUB. If costs increase, the AUB contribution will also increase and vice versa. And that has been the problem in the past, namely that it did not follow the level of activity. We are now constantly ensuring that there is a balance between expenditure and income, and both will have to be done by law. I think that the tripartite agreement, of which the bill is a partial implementation, means that much more of the pupils entering vocational training are completing their training and that we get far more of the highly sought-after skilled workers. ParlaMint-DK_20201215152542	Thank you. I wish I could make it as short as Mr. Knox. Ole Birk Olesen, but I don't think I can get away with that. I would like to thank all the rapporteurs for the comments that have been made. As you all know, and as everyone in the general public knows, we are still in a situation where corona has a major impact on everyday life for all of us, and unfortunately we must also recognise that the pandemic will still have consequences for both employees and employers at the beginning of the new year. That is why we are proposing today, with this legislative proposal, that we extend the three schemes in the field of employment, which would otherwise expire on 31 December of this year. Firstly, we propose to extend the right to sickness benefit for both people at increased risk and their relatives in the household. Of course, at the beginning of next year the risk group should not feel pressured to go to work and risk becoming seriously ill or having to quit their jobs in the difficult situation we are facing right now. Similarly, their loved ones should not be insecure about the possibility of bringing home infection after being at work. Secondly, we propose to extend the arrangements to ensure that parents who cannot work from home can receive maternity benefit on the days when they have to take care of their child at home because of corona. With the extension, we ensure that parents are not caught in a difficult situation where they must stay home from work and be concerned about losing their jobs or income. Thirdly, we propose to extend the suspension of the employer's period so that it can continue to help mitigate the financial consequences for employers who have employees affected by the corona virus. This means that employers can receive sickness benefit reimbursement from the first day of absence for the affected employees. The selfemployed persons covered will also be able to receive sickness benefit from the municipality from the first day of sickness. The draft law extends all three schemes in their current form until 31 March 2021, and I am convinced that with the extension of these schemes and the other aid packages that have been put in place, we will create the necessary security for employees and employers. The bill is therefore one of the pieces to help us get through the difficult situation we are facing. Thank you for the floor and thank you to the parties for the debate.

ParlaMint-DK_20201215153040	The sitting is resumed. The debate is open. The first to speak is the Socialist rapporteur, and that is Mr Beazley. Orla Sea.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215153101	Thank you for that, President, and thank you for the salomonian ten seconds. We're dealing with L 116, the Competition Act and the Criminal Code. The economic development of Denmark, yes, throughout the Western world, is largely based on healthy competition. This applies when technologies compete to be best, when materials are to be chosen, and when prices are to be set for a performance. Most parties share the view that competition is an important factor in the development and development of a society. There are various modulations of vision in healthy competition and its conditions. For social democracy, it is important that healthy competition is linked to fair and equal competition, and to ensure that fair and equal rules apply to competition, it is essential to ensure transparency and openness about the conditions of competition. We see L 116 as a clarification of the conditions governing competition. It is gratifying that the creation of L 116 is the European Union and its provisions, because it helps to ensure that competition rules apply to cross-border competition. When we have a strong desire for fair and equal conditions of competition combined with transparency, it is also desirable to be able to deal with the rules with reasonable powers for the authorities responsible for ensuring compliance with the competition rules. L 116 gives a number of powers: the holding of interviews, structural injunctions following court orders and, under certain conditions, the possibility of carrying out investigations in private homes. It is supplemented by the opening up of a civil fines system, in which companies and associations of undertakings may be subject to so-called civil fines in the context of the civil procedure, with due regard for the corresponding guarantees of legal certainty, as well as the additional guarantees of legal certainty which are applicable under Union law. L 116 lays down the rules on the independence already enjoyed by the Competition and Consumer Protection Authority and, in addition, provides for access to documents, mutual assistance to the competition authorities of other EU countries and adjusted rules on obsolescence. L 116 states that Denmark, like the other EU countries, applies the provisions of the directive both to cases under the rules of the TFEU and to cases under the Danish competition rules, which are in substance the same as the European ones. With social democratic spectacles, L 116 is a sensible anchoring of the conditions of competition. For competition, a healthy principle helps to ensure that citizens, consumers, businesses and society are the best solutions at the right price if they are based on equal and fair conditions. Social democracy supports the bill.

ParlaMint-DK_20201215155812	Thank you for the many remarks and for having received the bill in this Chamber. The overall aim of the draft law is to strengthen the powers of competition authorities to ensure the rapid and effective enforcement of competition rules. With the bill, we supplement the current powers of the Competition and Consumer Authority with the power to conduct interviews, to issue structural injunctions and to carry out checks in private homes under certain conditions. It is, of course, within the framework of the civil process and with due regard for the corresponding guarantees of legal certainty and the additional guarantees of legal certainty that apply under EU law. But I can say that we firmly believe that the Competition and Consumer Authority already has the independence required to comply with the directive. But let us at last get it further in the committee's proceedings. There is no reason to be insecure about it. Then the draft law provides for access to documents, mutual assistance to the competition authorities of other EU countries and adjusted rules on obsolescence. Finally, it has been proposed by Denmark and I can say to the parties of the Danish Parliament that it is also true in the other EU countries that the provisions of the directive apply both to cases under the rules of the TFEU and to the Danish competition rules, which are in substance the same as the European ones. There have also been some comments on the independence of the competition authorities. I think sir... Torsten Schack from the Left said that there has been a great deal of criticism, and I can inform the rapporteurs here that there have also been some adjustments to the draft law following the hearing, so that the criticism of legal certainty has been largely met. ParlaMint-DK_20201215160131	Thank you. I should like to begin by saying that I am giving this speech on behalf of my colleague, Financial Rapporteur Christian Rabjerg Madsen. The aim of the draft law, which we are considering at first reading today, is to amend the law so that the Presidency of the Economic Council is given the task of independently reviewing the assumptions underlying the ministries' estimates of the behaviour of economic policy measures. In addition, the draft law reduces the number of members of the Economic Council by two as a result of the merger of Dansk Industri and Dansk Byggeri as well as the merger of FTF and LO in the formation of the main trade union organisation. Furthermore, the draft law extends the number of members of the Environmental Economic Council as a result of the establishment of the Ministry of Food, Agriculture and Fisheries, and as the Ministry of the Environment and Food changes the designation to the Ministry of the Environment. When the government took office, the Minister of Finance announced that the principles of the Ministry of Finance should be assessed by independent professionals. The ongoing review must help to ensure that the accounting principles of the ministries are on the best possible professional basis and reflect the knowledge available, and that they are constantly updated as new knowledge becomes available on, for example, the value of investments in our welfare and in education. All parties in the Folketing, regardless of political convictions, have an interest in our being on the best possible and most up-to-date professional basis when making wise decisions. It is important that the review takes place independently, and that is why the Presidency of the Economic Council must decide for itself the details to be assessed and the order in which they are to be dealt with. The assessment may include recommendations for adjustments to the assumptions used, if warranted. The Minister for Finance will respond to the Presidency's recommendations, if any, on a follow-up or explaining principle, as is the case with the Presidency of the Economic Council's existing role as a fiscal watchdog. We are convinced that this action will strengthen both the professional debate and the knowledge base of the decisions to be taken here in the Folketing. And on the basis of this argument, the Social Democratic Party supports the bill as it stands here.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215160429	Thank you to the rapporteur. There are no questions. The next rapporteur is Mr Seligman. Christoffer Aagaard Melson, Left.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215160441	Thank you. I'm a substitute today for our financial rapporteur, sir. Troels Lund Poulsen. Thank you for the floor. Today we are discussing L 118, which proposes extending the role of the Economic Council and assigning them a new task of supervising the ministries. The task is, in short, that the Presidency of the Economic Council should carry out an independent examination of the assumptions underlying the estimates of the behaviour of the ministries. This means, more specifically, that when the Minister makes calculations of what, for example, an increase in taxation has to do with behavioural effects, the lookout will encourage that the estimates calculated are as accurate and up-to-date as possible. In the Left, we think that the draft law sounds like a really sensible approach, which can bring more openness to the accounting rules applied in the ministries. It is in the public interest to do what we can to ensure that transparency. We also fully support the follow-up or explanation model of the Ministry of Finance's response to any recommendations made by the Economic Council on the basis of audits carried out. Here it is proposed that the Ministry of Finance should either follow the recommendations or publicly explain why it chooses not to follow the recommendations. It will once again contribute to greater openness, and we can only support it when important and important decisions are taken, that we have that openness and have a second opinion if that word can be used. In conclusion, I would just like to say that the Liberal Party considers the draft law to be sensible and to the benefit of both openness and more precise estimates of the behavioural effects in the ministries. That is why the Left supports the proposal. Thank you for the floor.

These are measures relating, for example, to: public services, public consumption, public investment or taxes and charges, as stated in the comments to the legislative text. This bill extends the role of the wise men and gives them a new task of carrying out this review. In the context of the ongoing reporting, the Presidency may make any recommendations to the Minister for Finance. A similar principle applies to the Presidency's assessments of public finances. As I said, this is not as far-reaching a solution as we would have liked in the SF. ParlaMint-DK_20201215161905	Then we would like to thank the rapporteur. Then it is Mrs Jette Gottlieb, Enhedslisten, as rapporteur. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215161915	Thank you. This is the parade of temporary workers. I haven't met anyone in the Chamber yet, who are financial rapporteurs. I speak on behalf of Mr. Rune Lund and will therefore make his views known. We are discussing Bill L 118, which proposes to give the Presidency of the Economic Council the task of independently reviewing the assumptions underlying the ministries' estimates of the behaviour of economic policy measures. In the Enhedslisten we have long pointed out the need for a critical review of the calculation principles. We welcome the fact that the accounting principles of ministries regarding the behavioural effects of policy actions are being examined by an external party. We have documented in our 2018 report that in several cases the concrete calculation rules used by the Ministry of Finance to estimate behavioural effects are based on too thin or outdated documentation. This concerns, for example: changes in transfer incomes, changes in taxes, labour supply and employment, public consumption and privatisation. Overall, we concluded that there is such uncertainty in the Ministry's calculation rules that we recommended that the behavioural effects should no longer be calculated into revenue estimates in legislative proposals, reforms and the like. Instead, we recommended a precautionary principle either/or one could say. We are not opposed to the economic ministries making estimates of the behavioural effects of economic measures. However, until we are sure that there is a solid background, these estimates should be settled solely for the information provided by the Danish Parliament and thus qualify the Folketing's position on various measures. Such estimates should therefore not simply be included in advance in the estimates of revenue drawn up by the ministries for the purposes of legislative proposals, etc. We would have preferred to see a strengthening of Parliament's financial secretariat and a major reform review. Our proposal was, and continues to be, that it would be more appropriate to do four things in order to qualify the debate on the behavioural effects: firstly, to have a working party to evaluate major reforms carried out in recent years in order to determine whether the behavioural effects have actually occurred; secondly, a thorough review of the calculation methods by setting up a commission of economists from various economic schools; thirdly, a precautionary principle which does not include the effects directly in revenue estimates in legislative proposals. Fourthly, we want to strengthen Parliament's own economic muscles. We know this from other countries where the legislature has its own economic unit, which can provide insight and qualify the legislative process. This is appropriate because economists will then be employed, who are independent of the ministries and thus of the government. However, the draft law before us is a step forward. It gives the Presidency of the Economic Council the task of assessing the principles of calculation. This is a small and important step forward in our optics, although, as I said, we would have liked to have seen more solutions to the problem. An important element of this proposal is that the Presidency can involve external experts in the work of evaluating, according to Section 4 of the proposal on a new paragraph. 7. We believe that it would be very good to involve external experts to nuance the knowledge that exists on behavioural effects. An open and multidisciplinary approach to how people react to the decisions we politicians make will make us wiser and give a more truthful picture of reality. Unfortunately, we have to note, like the replies received, that the funds allocated to this task are likely to make it possible for the Presidency to obtain contributions from external experts only to a very limited extent. That is why we would like to see the commitment, which is targeted at the evaluation of calculation methods, be higher so that experts from outside can be involved in practice. It is also a step forward that the Presidency can point to the need to develop a new knowledge of methods of calculation and thereby make calls for the research environments. We also believe that such calls should focus on socio-scientific research environments broadly in order to get the best possible assessments into the picture. Overall, we believe that the draft law is a step forward that we can support. However, in the legislative process we will discuss with the parties of the Folketing and the government whether we should not set aside a little more funds for the task so that we can actually get the Presidency to involve external experts. This will strengthen the evaluation for the benefit of future legal work.

ParlaMint-DK_20201215171139	We thank the rapporteur. There is no one from the New Citizen, nor from the Liberal Alliance. Then it is the rapporteur for the authors of the motion, Mrs Susanne Zimmer. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215171152	Thank you. I would like to start by thanking you for the debate and for the good input and, in fact, a very positive attitude towards establishing these natural parks, and that it could possibly also go and become Han Herred. So I look forward to having it here in committee and finding a model so that we can get something going as soon as possible, and we need that, because right now we are in the sixth mass extinction of animals and plants. We can, among other things. look at the red list that is being made by Aarhus University, where the latest list shows that the animal being examined is 4.439 species corresponding to almost 42 per cent. of the evaluated species that are either extinct or in danger of disappearing. That figure is huge, and when you look at the fact that in 2010 it was in fact only half as large, that is to say on 2,262 animal species, then you get so worried. 62% of our endangered species of animals and plant species live in the forests, and in fact it is 44 per cent. of those who live only in the forests, so we know that we need space and also some of the space that is in the forests. That is why the Free Greens have tabled a motion for a resolution on a biodiversity law in order to create more space for nature. I'm afraid it didn't get any support. Now, instead, we have a package of nature and biodiversity, and that is good, but it is not good enough at all. For it will move the wild variety of nature from 1%. of land to 2%, which is very little. Nevertheless, we are pleased that the nature and biodiversity package includes 13 new national parks, because it is a step in the direction of giving nature more space. Now the question is: where are those parks to be located? In this motion for a resolution, the Free Greens are calling for the government to appoint a natural park in Jammerbugt, Han Herred National Park. Why are we doing this? There are many good reasons for this, and I will draw some of them out. It is an area with a very unique nature for both animal and plant species. In the last 50 years, 11 of our day butterfly species have disappeared, and in the area we intend to become a national park in Han Herred, there is a heatple day butterfly, and one third of the entire population is actually in this area. There are also birds that are threatened: large bobbins, reeds and natravs. Similarly, there are rare fungi and plant species. There are already large protected areas in this area. There are 3 areas and there are Natura 2000 sites. Some of the areas come together and overlap, and some lie separately, and it is precisely a problem that they lie separately because nature needs the big connections so that it can move around. Another problem is that these conservations are actually quite weak in relation to nature. That is to say, they do not protect as much as you could imagine they did. Then there is something else in that area, and it is that here we have Denmark's longest inland coastal cliff, Lien, at Fosdalen, and if you haven't seen it, I recommend you go up there and see it, because it's unique, beautiful nature, which you can't see anywhere else. Something else is that the only place in Denmark is a bird's mountain, and it's fantastic, and you can get very close to it and see it. So it is also an area that needs to be looked after, and not just that area, but also the area around it, because nature is connected. Then, as we have already mentioned, the majority of this area is, in fact, already state-owned. That is to say, it is easy to change, so you stop farming and you stop forestry because you cannot get into carambola with some owners, and it would be quite cheap to do so. Another thing is the engaging process that has been started in Jammerbugt, which was already started in May 2019, where you invited a wide range of organisations, stakeholders and citizens to a meeting and got a presentation from Rune Engelbeth about what a national park actually is, so people knew what they were going for. That meeting was received very positively, and there were really positive trends in terms of how you wanted it, and thus they also set in motion a process in relation to looking at different phases of how to make this happen. This is because the entire city council wants a national park. So, if we had been full-numbered here, one could say that of the parties that are in that town council, most are also represented here in the Folketing. It has also resulted in 2 million being set aside. kr. in the budget over the next two years. 2 million kr. may not seem so much in here in the Folketing, but in a sparsely populated rural municipality is 2 million. kr. A lot of money. They are also so privileged that they have an old refugee centre, which is actually very nice and very large and which they want to use as a communication centre, a place that you get through when you walk in these areas, and there are already certified hiking routes. The communication centre itself will operate so that you can allow people to enter and gain a greater theoretical knowledge of what they see, and the more knowledge and understanding you have of nature, the more you are keen to look after it, and we need it so much in the time we are in. As I said earlier, there is a process plan that can be set in motion directly, and thus it will be easy to start or at least take Han Herred Nature Park as one of the first projects. This will please nature, and we will stop forestry and farming, and we can expose the large grazing animals that promote insect life, which helps to spread seeds through their feces and which helps to graze the area so that it does not jump into forest, but it can continue to have some light openings. Then, of course, there must be a process going on, in relation to exactly where that area is and how it should be fenced, and that is one of the things that comes with being appointed as a national park. By appointing this Han Herred National Park you will also be able to give a sparsely populated area the opportunity to have even more tourists. In fact, many tourists are already taking advantage of this beautiful nature, but even more will be able to do so. It could also mean something to the local business community up there, because there are many small shops, farm shops, hotels, etc. that are actually helping to keep this tourism going. So in the Greens we think: what are we waiting for? In these words, I would like to commend the motion for a resolution and the related comments on the proposal for a favourable treatment of the matter. Thank you.

ParlaMint-DK_20201215172344	Thank you for the floor, President. This is, of course, an extremely topical issue which the questioner raises here. Faster processing times are high on the government's agenda. Legal certainty is, as was also said, not only to have its right, but also to have it within a reasonable period of time. In other words, it is crucial for the Danish legal system that we can quickly and consistently hold those who break the law accountable, just as we must be able to quickly and consistently help the victims who have been the victims of a crime. We owe this to both the accused and the victims so that they can put an end to a boring chapter and move forward in their lives. Justice is measured in time, as it were. I assume that the questioner is particularly interested in North Atlantic relations, as the speech also indicated, and that is why I would like to focus primarily on the measures aimed at the North Atlantic police forces. However, I would like to start by starting at home in Denmark, where we also have challenges, one can safely say, with processing times that are far too long. It has major consequences for both victims and defendants if they have to wait years for their cases to be dealt with. That is why the Code of Procedure states that both the prosecution, defence lawyers and the courts must promote criminal proceedings with the speed allowed by the nature of the case. In ordinary Danish this means that criminal proceedings are different and that it is not generally possible to say how long it is reasonable. This underlines how complex a challenge we face, but that does not mean that we should do nothing to try to solve that challenge. With the presentation of a new multi-year agreement for the police and prosecutor's office, the government is putting forward a focus on reducing the processing times, with concrete targets for how much we can have the processing times brought down. This must be done, first and foremost, by adding more hands to criminal proceedings in the police and the prosecution. I have no illusions that a snapshot solution can solve the challenges we face with long processing times. But it's one step on the way. As far as the two North Atlantic circles are concerned, it is important to remember that the police of the Faroe Islands and Greenland differ from the other Danish police circles. They do so on a number of points in terms of geography, infrastructure, assignments and the crime picture. That is why, in our proposal for a multi-annual agreement, we also have a special focus on the issues of the North Atlantic. First of all, the government proposes to set aside resources to deal with cases of sexual abuse against children in Greenland. We want to create a dedicated unit that can serve as a gateway to all cases of sexual abuse committed against children. It is our hope that this action, among other things, will be carried out. a. can contribute to a faster and more effective handling of cases. At the same time, it is our expectation that the work of deploying the police's case management system to the Nordic community can begin in the coming period of agreement if the technical prerequisites are to be put in place. With the roll-out of POLSAS, we will have the opportunity to establish a data base which will allow us to follow the process in the same way as in the 12 Danish circles, if I may say so. We actually miss that opportunity today. In addition to the initiatives included in the presentation of the multiannual agreement, the Ministry of Justice generally works to improve the procedures in Greenland and the Faroe Islands. Among other things: By amending the Greenlandic Code of Procedure in 2019, a legal basis was introduced for the appointment of a further Judge to the court in Greenland. The recruitment will greatly enable the Court in Greenland and the circuit courts to focus on shorter processing times. In addition, the Ministry of Justice has had a process with the Greenlandic judiciary with the aim of achieving a more flexible course throughout the criminal investigation chain. The process has been set in motion in continuation of discussions in the Council for the Law of Greenland. The work has shown that the processing times in Greenland, as in Denmark, are too long. This is particularly true in the period from a judgment in a case to an enforcement order. Following this, the Ministry of Justice has asked the Attorney General and the Court of Justice to examine the reasons for the long duration of proceedings and to assess whether concrete initiatives are necessary. The Ministry of Justice expects to be able to follow up work during the first half of 2021. At the same time, the government in the Finance Act 2020 has allocated funds for salary improvements to Greenlandic asylum officers and police officers. Among other things, it has to be done. contribute to enhanced recruitment. Finally, the Ministry of Justice generally has a major focus on constantly updating the legislation for both Greenland and the Faroe Islands, including special conditions to ensure better and more efficient processing. The Ministry of Justice has drawn up a bill which, among other things, is: aims to improve police reform for Greenland. I understand that the proposal is at first reading. The new Faroese Code of Criminal Procedure, which will enter into force on 1 January 2021, also implements the police reform for the Faroe Islands. Greenlandic and Faroese politics are thus treated in the same way as the other Danish police circles, which will strengthen the support of the police and the prosecution's work and thus also the proceedings in Greenland and the Faroe Islands. In addition, the Ministry of Justice is working on a draft law aimed at improving the effectiveness of criminal proceedings in Greenland, including: by introducing rules on the handling of confession proceedings and by extending the right to give judgment in default. The draft law must help to ensure a more effective settlement of cases where the defendant confesses or fails to do so. The discussion ends far from here in the Danish Parliament today. Reducing processing times and getting to the bottom of the big case piles is not something you do with a snap. It takes a long, cool move. This is also why we give priority to providing additional resources and focus on actions that can streamline the processing of cases in the authorities in the Ministry of Justice's area. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20201215182523	Thank you, President. Thank you, sir. Sjúrður Skaale to bring this question debate on a central issue into this Chamber. Of course, when a citizen commits a criminal act, he must be tried, punished, served his sentence and again be able to be resocialised in society. It is a crucial chain in our community of law. No legal certainty, no rule of law. The concept of legal certainty covers the guarantees that create the rule of law. Legal certainty ensures citizens against arbitrary, unpredictable abuses by the state. One of the key principles of the rule of law is that there is equality before the law. This means that the law applies in the same way to all, regardless of their social status. Another key principle is that anyone suspected of a crime is innocent until proven otherwise and must be brought before a competent and independent court. Therefore, the distance between crime and conviction must also be as short as possible. This is in the interests of the accused, and it is clearly also in the interests of the victims, and it is in the interests of the rule of law. I think it is a pity that we have recently seen criminals get a discount because of processing time. We cannot have that in a community of law, it hurts the sense of justice. That is why the rule of law also has a responsibility to identify the possibilities that can shorten and limit the temporal distance between accusation and the outcome of an action, that is to say, judgment or acquittal. So this is a very relevant debate we are having here. And part of what is being asked for is also being met in the ongoing police negotiations, and among other things. Therefore, we are Conservatives in the majority's proposal for adoption, recognising that the duration of the proceedings from notification to judgment is very central to legal certainty, and we note that the current agreement on the police and prosecutor's economy 2021-2024, among others. allocates resources for criminal proceedings in Denmark in order to reduce processing time. And not least, the paper also includes the police of the Faroe Islands and Greenland and addresses a number of the particular issues that arise in the Faroe Islands and Greenland respectively. So that is, I think, a fine proposal for adoption, and part of what is demanded is to see in the forthcoming police agreement. Thank you.

ParlaMint-DK_20201215185048	The debate is open. The first to speak is the Minister for Social Affairs and the Interior.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215185105	Thank you for that, President. Motion for a resolution B 58 seeks to cast doubt on the reform of compensation and, in particular, on the element of overcompensation. I think this is an excellent opportunity to visit again what the compensatory reform actually contains and has meant. First of all, I would like to remind you that the compensatory reform has been implemented as a broad political agreement. It is indeed a very good compensatory reform, in which we strengthen the opportunity to provide welfare in all municipalities and to have a fairer and simpler system. It is very gratifying that such a comprehensive reform is based on such a broad majority in the Folketing. This creates credibility about the equalisation system and stability in the local economy for many years to come. With the reform, a number of well-known imbalances in the system have been corrected and we are redistributing more from the most prosperous municipalities to the most vulnerable municipalities. The reform has moved as much as 1.4 billion. kr. to the country's 30 outlying municipalities annually. As part of the increased redistribution from the well-off to the most vulnerable municipalities, the compensatory reform has introduced a ceiling on how much the most prosperous municipalities can get at a discount in the so-called over-compensation scheme. We have done so because we do not think it is reasonable that the most prosperous municipalities, with the country's lowest municipal tax rates, should have a huge discount on their contributions to the compensation. I'll get back to that. As part of the compensation agreement, we have ensured a fair transition to the new system of transitional arrangements and an opportunity for municipalities with losses to increase income tax equivalent to their losses without the risk of individual penalties under the rules of the Tax Penal Code. At the same time, we have agreed a similar framework for subsidies for tax reductions, giving priority to the municipalities that today have the highest tax rate. In this way, the reform also helps to reduce the difference in the municipal tax that the Danes pay across the country. These are some of the various elements of the reform that together mean that we have got a fairer balance. The motion for a resolution refers to an extra and hidden compensation. It is a somewhat distorted picture of reality and the agreement that a broad majority in the Folketing is behind. There's nothing extra or hidden. The effect referred to is the so-called over-compensation system. The excess compensation scheme means that, in particular, the most prosperous municipalities with a low tax rate receive a discount in their contribution to the other municipalities in the compensation. The excess compensation was found in the compensation before the compensatory reform. And there has been a political desire for this system to continue with the compensatory reform, but there has also been a political desire to limit the rebate. This government does not consider it fair, as I said, that municipalities with a municipal tax far below the national average should be able to receive an ever greater rebate in their compensation, the longer they reduced the tax, as was the case in the old system. Therefore, it has been a central element of the compensatory reform that a ceiling should be set for the rebate in the scheme, and it has been included in the calculated effects of the reform again nothing concealed, but a deliberate political priority that has been aimed at creating a fairer equalisation system, where there is affording proper well-being in all the municipalities of the country. I shall be the first to admit that over-compensation is not the easiest element to understand in the compensation system, but I am convinced that the municipalities that have had and still benefit from over-compensation are fully involved. The continuation of the scheme has thus also had an impact on the impact of the compensatory reform. This is particularly true for a number of northern Zealand municipalities, which could have suffered much greater losses without a continuation. But let us look a little more at the technique: the equalisation of the municipal tax base is based on an average tax base. The compensation scheme basically operates in such a way that municipalities with a high tax base and a low tax rate receive a calculated reduction in compensation equal to at least 7%. of the tax revenue calculated by the actual printing rate of the municipality. The lower a municipality in the compensation oversets the printing rate, the greater the discount it can thus obtain under the scheme in the municipality's contribution to the compensation. However, with the compensatory reform, a ceiling on the over-compensation rebate has been introduced, which means that even if a municipality's printing rate is lower than the ceiling of 23.25 per cent, it can only receive a discount equivalent to that it can keep up to 7%. of the proceeds calculated with a printing of 23,25%. This means that a municipality cannot obtain a higher discount by setting the tax rate below 23.25%, as was the case in the old system. At the same time, it also means that municipalities with a tax below the ceiling of 23.25%. will have an unchanged over compensatory rebate, even if it had to raise the tax up to and including the ceiling. Before the compensatory reform, where there was no ceiling, any increase in the income tax of municipalities in excess compensation would reduce the over-compensation rebate calculated. As I said, it has been an important priority for the compensatory reform to be successfully phased in. In this context, municipalities with losses have been given the opportunity to raise income tax correspondingly their losses without the risk of individual penalties which otherwise normally apply to tax increases. It is important to stress here that the revenue generated by the municipalities in the event of an increase in taxes is calculated with a correction for, among other things, the following: the impact on any excess compensation paid by the municipality. The municipalities which have been given a framework to raise the tax from 2021 have thus been able to determine the tax, so that the loss resulting from the compensatory reform can be covered by the net revenue obtained after taking into account the derived effect on the excess compensation. This means that municipalities have been able to cover the loss in excess compensation rebate that they may have by raising the tax. I can add that these are municipalities such as Rudersdal, Gentofte and Hørsholm, which continue to have the lowest printing rates in the country. If we look at the tax determination for 2021, tax increases within the allocated framework have given rise to an over-compensation rebate of a total of 48 million. kr., which the municipalities in question have thus covered through the tax increase. I would like to point out that the part of the tax increase in 2021 that can be attributed to a phased loss in the compensatory reform has only reduced the over-compensation debate by a total of approx. 3 million kr. for the relevant municipalities. However, several of the municipalities in Northern Zealand wanted to have the opportunity to raise the tax more than what needed to cover the loss in the compensatory reform. They have sought and taken part in a residual framework for further increases. It is these additional tax increases, as a result of their share in the residual amount, that are the reason for virtually all the fall in the over-compensation debate. It is against this background the government's view that there is no basis for compensating the most prosperous municipalities for lost discounts under the overcompensation scheme. A broad majority in the Folketing agree on an overall equalisation agreement that creates a fairer equalisation system. It has always been the intention to introduce a ceiling on the rebate in excess of compensation, that the most prosperous municipalities with the lowest tax rates should not have a special discount on their contributions to the compensation, which increased, the more they reduced the tax. The proposal to compensate the municipalities in question is equivalent to rolling back part of the compensatory reform, and that would also mean that we would have to grant a permanent extra subsidy to the municipalities in the country that have the highest tax base. The government will not be involved in this, and that is why we reject the motion for a resolution.

It is not fair for municipalities with a municipal tax far below the average to receive an increasing rebate in their compensatory contributions and the longer the tax is reduced. ParlaMint-DK_20201215195205	In May of this year, the Left and the Socialists agreed on a compensatory reform. The so-called rich municipalities already pay billions in compensation, and the discussion here is not about whether or not there should be compensation. It is about whether there should be more equalisation in relation to the enormous equalisation that is already taking place. A municipality like Gentofte already pays 3.3 billion. kr. in compensation per year to other municipalities. That's 43,000 kroner. per citizens, then an ordinary family in Gentofte • two adults, two children • pay each year what is equivalent to a medium-sized car for people in other municipalities. Every year, a municipality pays that amount. The government and the Left think so should be even more. It is not enough for every citizen of Gentofte to pay 43,000 kroner. a year. When Gentofte Municipality then raises the tax, it happens absurd that the extra tax will also be offset. If Gentofte raises the tax by 100 million. kr. It can raise the tax a little, but if it raises the extra amount then there will be only 60 million. kr. in the municipal treasury. So the municipality gets a bill, and if it tries to pay the bill by levying more taxes from its own citizens, then the state also takes a large part of this extra municipal tax. We think that even for the government and also for the Left, there must be a limit to how much we are in favour of raising taxes. The Left prides itself on having a principle of a tax freeze, and therefore the Left should not be in favour of such a violent extra taxation of people in municipalities as Gentofte, Rudersdal and Lyngby-Taarbæk. The Left and the Government have made an unfair compensatory reform, but we hope, at least, that the Left and other parties will vote in favour of our motion for a resolution here that municipalities that have to levy more taxes due to the compensatory reform should not be subject to double taxation, by the fact that the extra tax is also being offset.

ParlaMint-DK_20201215201521	The debate is open. The word is given to the Minister for Social Affairs and the Interior.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215201528	Thank you. I am now sure that we could find a way to facilitate some committee handling over in the Ministry if we failed here in the Folketing. There's always coffee at our place. I just want you to know that. Thank you for the floor and thank you for the debate on something as important as the municipal economy and our equalisation system. This motion for a resolution, B 59, deals with municipal tax increases for 2021, where the Conservative People's Party will impose on the government that the statutory offsetting of a minor tax overrun for 2021 should not be implemented. I must therefore understand that the Conservative People's Party approves of the fact that the municipalities increase taxes in addition to the established framework for the tax discharge. It's new tones from that edge. It is precisely the case that the municipalities have already been guaranteed flexibility in the tax reduction in the context of the compensatory reform. There is a framework for tax increases in some municipalities and subsidies for reductions in other municipalities, but it is worth noting that there is a requirement for an overall unchanged tax. The tax framework we now see has helped to reduce the differences in what citizens in different parts of the country have to pay for welfare. In this way, the compensatory reform has, in more than one way, contributed to more equality. Perhaps it is also more dissatisfaction with the compensatory reform of the Conservative People's Party that is actually the reason for today's debate. A broad political majority is behind the reform of the compensation system that we carried out in the spring. The agreement means, among other things, that 1.4 billion will be redistributed. kr. more to the country's outermost municipalities, which is good, while the most prosperous municipalities now contribute more. The agreement contains several elements which together mean that we have got a fairer distribution of the economy across the country. One of the elements is the treasure. The tax framework agreed as part of the compensatory reform allows municipalities to adapt to the new system. It is therefore possible for municipalities, with losses in the compensatory reform, to increase their tax burden as they phase in their losses. The possible tax increase is determined on the basis of the municipality's losses on the reform resulting from the political agreement with a broad majority behind it, and not on the basis of the municipalities' possible alternative estimate of the loss. At the same time, in order that the reform should not lead to overall tax increases, we have agreed on a similar framework for tax reductions. Here we have wanted to give priority to the municipalities which today have the highest tax. These are attractive grant schemes in which the 12 municipalities, which in 2020 have a tax rate of over 26.3 per cent, are permanently granted 100 per cent. in subsidies to reduce the tax to 23.6%. For other tax reductions, the grant in the first year amounts to as much as 90 per cent. the loss of revenue and then it is phased out over a 5-year period. As mentioned above, some municipalities have other factors which they considered to be a loss but which are not a consequence of the compensatory reform. This applies in particular to the disappearance of the two temporary two-year transitional arrangements established in 2018 as a result of a revision of the Statistics Denmark's education statistics and a more precise statement of the age-related expenditure requirements in the area of the elderly. The revised data base corrected the fact that some municipalities had been systematically overcompensated for their expenditure needs. In other words, they had been compensated for a level of expenditure they did not have. But this is precisely where these transitional arrangements were temporary. They were given for a two-year period so that the municipalities concerned had time to adapt to the new circumstances. The two transitional arrangements would expire in 2021, regardless of the reform. The fact that individual municipalities have not taken due care and adapted to it in the past has nothing to do with the compensatory reform. Nevertheless, the parties behind the reform have been sensitive to a desire for further flexibility in raising taxes in the municipalities, which, in addition to major losses to the compensatory reform, also have the greatest losses at the end of the two transitional arrangements. That is why an amendment to the Equalisation Act was tabled which made it possible to allocate a residual framework for increases to the few municipalities with significant losses on both the compensatory reform and the expiry of two transitional arrangements. In this way the government has together with among others. The left and the other parties behind the countervailing agreement have already taken account of the expiry of these transitional arrangements. Here again, I would like to remind you that we are talking about municipalities that have been overcompensated in the past and have known that this overcompensation would expire in 2021 with the end of the transitional arrangements. The municipalities have therefore had time to adapt to a situation without transitional arrangements. The result of the municipalities' tax determination for 2021 is that many municipalities have changed income tax. It is especially municipalities with a low tax that have had to contribute more to the compensation that has increased the tax, and at the same time it is especially municipalities with a high tax that have reduced it with subsidies. It is especially the outermost municipalities that have put the tax down. This means that ordinary citizens in less well-off peripheral municipalities no longer have to pay such a high municipal tax, while citizens in the more prosperous municipalities have to pay a tax that is slightly closer to the average than before. I am very pleased and proud that this has significantly reduced the difference between the highest and lowest municipal taxes. In 2020, the difference between the highest and lowest tax rates is 5 percentage points. In 2021 we will see a difference that is down to 3.5 percentage points. This is a positive development, which is also what we wanted with the agreed tax measures in the compensatory reform. In view of the extent to which changes in taxes have taken place, tax overruns of approx. 126 million kr. is considered to be a minor deviation from the assigned framework. The excess of the 126 million kr. are penalised in accordance with the rules in force by offsetting the block subsidy, which the Conservative People's Party has also contributed to in the past when they sat in government offices. The discount brings the revenues of the municipalities back to the level provided for in the agreement with KL on the municipalities' economy for 2021. A set-off therefore does not affect the consumption framework of the municipalities, for which there is, of course, full funding in the economic agreement. I am not only surprised that the Conservative People's Party wants to approve an overall increase in municipal tax by cancelling the set-off, but also that this is justified by the expiry of the previous transitional arrangements. For as mentioned above, tax frameworks have already been allocated on the basis of the expiry of these transitional arrangements, well to mark the transitional arrangements given for the phasing-out of overcompensation, the expiry of which after 2 years was known. Neither the expiry of the transitional arrangements nor the alternative perceptions of the loss resulting from the compensatory reform are essential reasons for cancelling the otherwise statutory offsetting of the excess tax. I also wonder, as I said, whether the reason for the proposal is not that a compensatory reform has been adopted, which means that a number of well-off municipalities must contribute more to equalisation. The motion for a resolution has therefore given no good reason why an excess over what was agreed means that the tax should not be offset, as has been the case in previous years and in the years under the bourgeois government, of which the Conservative People's Party itself was a part. Despite the slight overrun, I would like to maintain that the tax result is a positive story, because the tax spread between municipalities with high taxes and municipalities with low taxes has diminished. This is a result which, together with the increased redistribution in the new equalisation system, contributes to more equality between municipalities and citizens across the country.

ParlaMint-DK_20201215204336	Our motion for a resolution here is about the fact that there are other changes to the economy of the municipalities than the compensation agreement that mainly the Left and the Social Democrats made in May. It cannot be right that you should be punished twice, so you have to pay an extra bill, and then when you try to pay it and have to raise the tax, you are punished even more, and that is why we have this proposal. We think we could undo that, for example. a municipality such as Lyngby-Taarbæk, if there is any reason to do so, but I have noted that there is rather little support for this in the Assembly Chamber, and that disappoints us, but we are not surprised, because we know, as we know, that there has been a reform here which was aimed at taking from certain municipalities. ParlaMint-DK_20201215205102	The debate is open. First it's the tax minister. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215205115	Thank you for that, President. And thank you very much for the opportunity to discuss an important subject. Today we are dealing with a resolution by the Conservative People's Party on smoking and, in particular, on how we can reduce smoking by bringing forward the tax increase on cigarettes and introducing rules on what is also called clean market. The Conservative People's Party proposes to bring forward the planned tax increase, which is equivalent to DKK 5. for a package of 20 cigarettes, from 1 January 2022 to 1 January 2021. It is also proposed that cigarettes with old stamp marks may no longer be sold in the retail trade as from 1 February, which is equivalent to one month after the increase. With the proposal, the Conservative People's Party wishes to ensure compliance with the political intentions that tobacco products should be more expensive with the aim of reducing the number of smokers, including in particular preventing children and young people from starting smoking. I would like to start by saying, as I think has also been very clear from the discussions we have had and from the initiatives taken by the government, that the government cannot agree more with the intentions behind this proposal. It has also emerged from the two consultations that we had on this subject, where the subject is, of course, one of them. I have also made a very clear statement on my position on the substance of the proposal. That is also why the government is already in full swing to pave the way for the tobacco tax increase to become a reality. In the 2020 Finance Act, we increased tobacco taxes because, as a government, we want fewer people to start smoking, especially the desire for fewer children and young people to start smoking. If, now, months after the increase, you can still find cigarettes with an old, low price, yes, it goes, by its very nature, against that purpose. What we have seen is that tobacco producers are now working directly against the purpose of the increase. They have dumped prices and ordered stamp marks at a value which means that they simply lose money on their sales of cigarettes. As Minister for Taxation, I must admit that I think it is both gross but also directly unsavory that the intention of the legislation on which we agree here in the Folketing is not respected. The government has therefore recently launched a plan of action with seven actions, including: concerns taxes on tobacco and nicotine as well as stricter requirements and increased controls on tobacco trade. I will not go into the individual actions of the action plan now, as I think that for those present in the Chamber, the issues are gradually becoming known. But we will be discussing them during the spring. So as far as the specific proposal to bring forward the tax increase is concerned, it is simply not possible to allow it to enter into force as from 1 January 2021. This is due to the fact that the advance will, in practice, require a change of law, which, according to the normal procedure, will not be possible before 1 January 2021. And even if the legislation itself could be put in place, there is of course also a need for some implementation of the changes. This applies both to the tax authorities, to the stamps and, of course, to the companies themselves. A possible advance of the planned tax increase is estimated not to take effect until April 2021. And, of course, this time will have to be consolidated by the Danish Tax Office, which is responsible for the practical implementation. And they have previously estimated that they and companies need at least 3 months to change a tax like this. It is also important to note that it is indeed necessary to bring forward the increase in the tax on cigarettes, which amounts to approximately 130 million. kr. for 1 year. Perhaps it seems a little illogical that raising a tax would actually cost money, but this is because the Danes, among other things. border trade will increase the number of cigarettes, which is why the increase will result in less revenue for the State. Last but not least, I must point out, for the sake of good order, that it is the tax department's opinion that an advance can not only include finished cigarettes but must also include smoking tobacco. Smoking tobacco is available in two variants: the fine-cut, which is used for roll self-cigarettes, and the coarsely cut, which is used primarily for pipe smoking, but which can also, if the price difference is too high, be used for roll self-cigarettes. Smoking tobacco is therefore directly substituteable with cigarettes. With regard to the proposal that cigarettes may only be sold with old stamps for one month after the tax increase, I would point out that the government has proposed in its action plan that there should be a so-called clean market after three months. It is not because the government wants to be kind to cigarette manufacturers, rather, on the contrary, but because the Ministry of Taxation considers that both tobacco traders, tobacco producers and tax authorities should have a reasonable time to implement such a rule. A shorter period should be expected to significantly increase the administrative burden on all parties, and I would also like to point out that smoking tobacco has a lower turnover and is therefore longer on shelves than cigarettes. They will therefore be hit extra hard by a clean market rule after only 1 month. In addition, the very short period is not expected to allow companies sufficient time to adapt production, and therefore the proposal can be expected to give an economic loss to tobacco producers if, for example, they do not have sufficient time to adapt production. should choose to distribute the tobacco products rather than return the used stamp marks as they do. As I said, the government strongly supports the intentions behind the proposal. We will be discussing further steps on how to get a better and more firm framework on this whole issue and, as I have said, we have presented a plan of action which already includes a proposal on the so-called clean market. There's no doubt that we're going to look at this. I must admit that I am surprised at the behaviour I have seen after I have been allowed to sit in the chair of the Minister of Taxation, particularly on the part of tobacco producers, after we have been and fundamentally agree on the framework for this market for a long time before the parliamentary elections, during the parliamentary election campaign and after the parliamentary elections. I have to say that I am surprised that in this way the intention is so strongly opposed to dumping prices, thereby challenging the framework laid down in very broad agreement here in the Danish Parliament. That is why we need a different path, and the government hopes that this will be based on the action plan we have put forward. There are other parties that have also had good ideas. I hope we can find each other in the spring and put even firmer frameworks around this. Thank you for the floor.

In the Left we support the motion for a resolution B 90, and we will support the work of the committee for a speedy examination, and we are aware that we are approaching New Year's Eve and it may prove impossible to keep to the timetable set out in the resolution. But then we will have to see in another way whether we cannot introduce the ambitions of the Conservative People's Party resolution as soon as possible.

However, if it is at the level of tax regulation where, at the same time, all the adults will be affected and where the young people can really just buy the cigarettes from those who negotiate them illegally, no, then I do not think it is the right way to go, and I do not think that will have that effect. So it should be borne in mind that the development has been really, really positive, and probably it would have been, even if nothing had been done at all. Otherwise there would have to be a strange development, that is, suddenly it would just be constantly increasing for 10 years to come, and I really do not think so. Because we have seen a nice fall, and I actually think that if you had just continued, you would have seen it too.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215215056	We thank the rapporteur. There are no more brief comments. Then it is the rapporteur for the authors of the motion, sir. Per Larsen, Conservative People's Party.
ParlaMint-DK_20201215215105	Thank you for that, President, and thank you to Parliament's parties for the debate here. As a party in the Conservative People's Party, we have no intention whatsoever that it should be more expensive to be a Dane, not at all, but smoking kills, which is why children should of course not smoke. That's how short it can be said. Fortunately, there is also broad agreement on this here in the Folketing. Most parties are actively involved in this effort, and I would also like to thank you for the fine work we have done together in connection with the national action plan against smoking by children and young people. After all, it is about smoke-free school hours, smoke-free cadastres at educational institutions, advertising bans, cigarette packets, stepping up controls on age requirements for the purchase of cigarettes and many other good initiatives to pave the way for a smoke-free youth year. So it is a very good day today because we have just voted through the proposal with the national action plan. However, we also know that the price of cigarettes is the most crucial parameter in relation to whether children and young people start smoking and therefore the price must be high. We have also tried, and the goal was that the price this year should rise to 55 DKK. and then rise to 60 kroner. from the turn of the year 2021-22 for a package of cigarettes, but that has not been the case with the good intentions of the majority in Parliament, because the creativity of the tobacco industry is impressive. Price war simply broke out on the cigarette market, and cigarettes were sold at child-friendly prices by 2020. The fact that producers have sold with losses simply to cultivate and maintain people in their dependence and cultivate the market is sad and shameful. This shows quite clearly that when you sell cigarettes at below the tax price, it is a fantastic good market they have here and there is a great deal of money. But that meant that instead of the 55 kroner. you could see prices in the vicinity of 40 DKK. the package. This means that there is plenty of room to raise the price by the fives which this proposal is proposing, and we think it might as well be done so that we get to the level that it ends up with. Let me remind you that 40 children start smoking every day with these low prices. One in three of them will die from tobacco-related diseases if they become dependent on tobacco smoke and continue to smoke. If, on the other hand, we prevent children and young people from starting to smoke, yes, we can avoid 75,000 cases of cancer, 70,000 cases of col and 60,000 cases of cardiovascular disease in relation to the calculations made by the Ministry of Health. I believe that we, as legislators, have a great responsibility both to ensure that we have a smoke-free youth and vintages that can have a healthier and better life, but also in relation to our health services and the capacity that is, after all, used on tobacco-derived diseases. So our intention will be to raise prices as quickly as possible and thus to achieve it. But fortunately, we're going to finish with the 60 kroner, probably. It will then turn out, because now we have become wiser, and we know that there is some elasticity and there are good margins for making cigarettes, but at some point we will probably get up there. Of course, I had hoped that a majority could be created and that the tax minister and the government were in favour of this proposal, but unfortunately that does not seem to be the case, and then we will have to put the proposal to the vote. However, I welcome the fact that, although it is a little late, we have at least increased tax increases in the future. Thank you for the floor.
